.
Notably, the magnitudes of the reduced angular power spectra decrease as
A_{\mathrm{S}}
increases.
By mirroring each contour plot of the first row along the axis of
H_{\mathrm{NL}}=0
and comparing it with the contour plot in the third row, we observe that the two corresponding plots are not entirely identical.
The differences become more pronounced as
A_{\mathrm{S}}
increases, indicating that the sign degeneracy between
F_{\mathrm{NL}}
and
H_{\mathrm{NL}}
in
h^{2}\bar{\Omega}_{\mathrm{gw},0}(\nu)
is broken in
\ell(\ell+1)\tilde{C}_{\ell}(\nu)
, particularly for larger values of
A_{\mathrm{S}}
.
Furthermore, it is notable that the position of the largest magnitude in
\ell(\ell+1)\tilde{C}_{\ell}(\nu_{\ast})
is close to that of the smallest magnitude in each contour plot.
These positions occur within the ranges where both
F_{\mathrm{NL}}H_{\mathrm{NL}}
and
G_{\mathrm{NL}}
are negative, and
H_{\mathrm{NL}}A_{\mathrm{S}}^{3/2}
remains relatively small.

Figure 14
:
Comparison between the theoretical angular power spectra and the noise angular power spectra of Laser Interferometer Space Antenna, Taiji and their networks at the milli-Hz band
Zhao and Wang (2024)
, as well as Deci-hertz Interferometer Gravitational wave Observatory at the deci-Hz band
Capurri et al. (2023)
. Shaded regions represent the uncertainties (68% confidence level) due to the cosmic variance, i.e.,
\Delta C_{\ell}/C_{\ell}=\sqrt{2/(2\ell+1)}
.

Within the relevant parameter regimes, the anisotropies in scalar-induced gravitational waves can potentially be detected at low multipoles by upcoming space-borne gravitational wave detectors, such as Laser Interferometer Space Antenna, Taiji, and Deci-hertz Interferometer Gravitational wave Observatory.

Fig.

14
presents a comparison of the angular sensitivity curves of these gravitational wave detectors with the theoretical angular power spectra of scalar-induced gravitational waves at the pivot frequency
\nu_{\ast}
as a function of multipoles.
Notably, the sensitivity regime of Laser Interferometer Space Antenna and Taiji falls within the milli-Hz band, while that of Deci-hertz Interferometer Gravitational wave Observatory operates in the deci-Hz band.
For the angular power spectra of scalar-induced gravitational waves at the frequency
\nu=\nu_{\ast}
, we set
A_{\mathrm{S}}=10^{-2}
, which is relevant to scenarios involving primordial black hole formation
Carr et al. (2021)
, and vary the primordial non-Gaussianity parameters.
The shaded regions along each angular power spectrum curve represent uncertainties due to cosmic variance at the
68\%
confidence level.
It is observed that the angular power spectra of scalar-induced gravitational waves can be detected by Laser Interferometer Space Antenna and Taiji individually at
\ell\leq 5
, and their combined efforts can more effectively assess the properties of scalar-induced gravitational waves, potentially detecting their anisotropies up to
\ell\leq 19
.
Although the anisotropies in scalar-induced gravitational wave under the Gaussian scenario exceed the detection capabilities of Laser Interferometer Space Antenna and Taiji, they could be probed by the more advanced Deci-hertz Interferometer Gravitational wave Observatory.

5
Non-Gaussianity in the SIGW background

In this section, we extend our investigation to the non-Gaussianity in the scalar-induced gravitational wave background, characterized by the higher-order statistics of the density contrast.
We focus on the three- and four-point correlation functions that define the angular bispectrum and trispectrum, respectively.
While the angular power spectrum studied in the previous section refers to the covariance of energy density fluctuations superimposed on the scalar-induced gravitational wave background, the angular bispectrum and trispectrum serve as natural extensions of skewness and kurtosis for random fluctuations.
Consequently, these spectra are fundamental quantities representing the non-Gaussianity in the anisotropic scalar-induced gravitational wave background, providing valuable insights into the nature of the early Universe.

5.1
Angular bispectrum: three-point angular correlation

As is customary in cosmic microwave background studies, we define the scalar-induced gravitational wave angular bispectrum
Bartolo et al. (2020a)
for the rotation-invariant gravitational wave background through the three-point correlator of
\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0,\ell m}(2\pi\nu)
, denoted as

\displaystyle\left\langle\prod_{i=1}^{3}\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0,\ell_{i}m_{i}}(2\pi\nu)\right\rangle=\begin{pmatrix}\ell_{1}&\ell_{2}&\ell_{3}\\
m_{1}&m_{2}&m_{3}\end{pmatrix}B_{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\ell_{3}}(\nu)\ ,

(67)

where the matrix enclosed in parentheses labels the Wigner 3-
j
symbol.

3
3

3

It is also common to define the reduced angular bispectrum in an alternative manner
Komatsu and Spergel (2001)
;
Ade and others (2014)
;
Bartolo et al. (2020a)
, denoted as

\displaystyle\left\langle\prod_{i=1}^{3}\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0,\ell_{i}m_{i}}(2\pi\nu)\right\rangle=\mathcal{G}_{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\ell_{3}}^{m_{1}m_{2}m_{3}}\tilde{b}_{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\ell_{3}}(\nu)\ ,

where the Gaunt integral
\mathcal{G}_{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\ell_{3}}^{m_{1}m_{2}m_{3}}
, defined later in Eq. (
69
), ensures the assumptions of statistical isotropy and parity invariance.
Compared to the definition provided in Eq. (
67
), this formulation factorizes
h_{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\ell_{3}}
, thereby ensuring parity invariance with
h_{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\ell_{3}}=0
for odd parity.

Furthermore, it is important to note that the tetrahedral domain of multipole triplets must satisfy both the triangular inequalities (
\ell_{1}\leq\ell_{2}+\ell_{3}
,
\ell_{2}\leq\ell_{1}+\ell_{3}
,
\ell_{3}\leq\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}
, and
m_{1}+m_{2}+m_{3}=0
), which are upheld by the Wigner 3-
j
symbols.
A brief overview of the properties of Wigner 3-
j
symbols is presented in Appendix
A
.
Additionally, parity invariance imposes the condition (
\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}+\ell_{3}=2n
, where
n\in\mathbb{N}
).
These conditions are also consistent with those of the cosmic microwave background angular bispectrum
Ade and others (2014)
;
Komatsu (2001)
.

Figure 15
:
Feynman-like diagrams for the three-point correlator of
\omega_{\mathrm{gw}}(\eta_{\mathrm{in}},\mathbf{k},\mathbf{q})
at leading order in
A_{\mathrm{L}}
.

The angular bispectrum quantifies the mixed central third-order moment, corresponding to skewness, of the fluctuations on the scalar-induced gravitational wave background.
Previous studies have provided relevant insights into primordial non-Gaussianity at
F_{\mathrm{NL}}
order
Bartolo et al. (2020a)
;
Bartolo et al. (2020b)
, extending to
G_{\mathrm{NL}}
order
Li et al. (2024a)
.
In this work, we extrapolate this analysis to include primordial non-Gaussianity up to all orders, offering a specific expression for primordial non-Gaussianity at
H_{\mathrm{NL}}
order.
The methodology for studying the angular bispectrum parallels that employed for investigating the angular power spectrum in Section
4
.
The calculation of the angular bispectrum, denoted as
B_{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\ell_{3}}
, involves the three-point correlator of the initial energy-density full spectrum, expressed as
\langle\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}\rangle
.
Based on
Fig.

1
and
Fig.

3
, we construct the Feynman-like diagrams for this three-point correlator by connecting three diagrams of
\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}
using “non-Gaussian bridges”.
To determine the leading-order connected contribution in
A_{\mathrm{L}}
, it is crucial to minimize the presence of “non-Gaussian bridges” in the diagrams.
As shown in
Fig.

15
, we consider only the diagrams with two “non-Gaussian bridges”, corresponding to the connected contribution at
\mathcal{O}(A_{\mathrm{L}}^{2})
order, while excluding diagrams with a higher number of “non-Gaussian bridges”.
Therefore, we aim to evaluate the three-point correlator
\langle\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(2)}\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(1)}\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(1)}\rangle
, with the expressions for
\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(1)}
and
\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(2)}
provided in Eq. (
27
) and Eq. (
28
), respectively.

We provide a concise overview of the derivation process for the explicit expression of
B_{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\ell_{3}}(\nu)
here.
Following the decomposition utilizing spherical harmonics, as demonstrated in Eqs. (
58
), we substitute
\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0,\ell m}^{(1)}
and
\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0,\ell m}^{(2)}
into the three-point correlator, resulting in an integral for
B_{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\ell_{3}}(\nu)
as outlined in Eq. (
67
).
Within the integrand, the correlator simplifies to
\langle\zeta_{\mathrm{gL}}(\mathbf{p})\zeta_{\mathrm{gL}}(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{p})\zeta_{\mathrm{gL}}(\mathbf{k}_{2})\zeta_{\mathrm{gL}}(\mathbf{k}_{3})\rangle\sim\delta^{(3)}(\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{2}+\mathbf{k}_{3})(2\pi^{2}\Delta^{2}_{\mathrm{L}})^{2}/(k_{2}k_{3})^{3}+2\,\text{permutations}
, and the exponential factor
e^{i\mathbf{k}_{1}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{2}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{2}+\mathbf{k}_{3}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{3}}
transforms to
\prod_{i=1}^{3}e^{-i(\mathbf{k}_{i}\cdot\mathbf{n}_{i})(\eta_{0}-\eta_{\mathrm{in}})}
due to the line-of-sight relation and momentum conservation.
Furthermore, the Fourier counterpart of the momentum-conservation factor reads

\delta^{(3)}(\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{2}+\mathbf{k}_{3})=\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}\mathbf{r}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\,\prod_{i=3}^{3}e^{i\mathbf{k}_{i}\cdot\mathbf{r}}\ ,

(68)

which allows us to express it in terms of an exponential function.
By combining this with the previously mentioned exponential factor, we utilize the identity in Eq. (
60
) to express both in terms of spherical harmonics and spherical Bessel functions.
To address the multiple spherical harmonics present in the integral, we leverage their properties and introduce the Gaunt integral as follows

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\ell_{3}}^{m_{1}m_{2}m_{3}}=\int\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{n}\,Y_{\ell_{1}m_{1}}(\mathbf{n})Y_{\ell_{2}m_{2}}(\mathbf{n})Y_{\ell_{3}m_{3}}(\mathbf{n})=h_{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\ell_{3}}\begin{pmatrix}\ell_{1}&\ell_{2}&\ell_{3}\\
m_{1}&m_{2}&m_{3}\end{pmatrix}\ ,

(69)

where
h_{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\ell_{3}}
is defined as

h_{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\ell_{3}}=\sqrt{\frac{(2\ell_{1}+1)(2\ell_{2}+1)(2\ell_{3}+1)}{4\pi}}\begin{pmatrix}\ell_{1}&\ell_{2}&\ell_{3}\\
0&0&0\end{pmatrix}\ .

(70)

It is important to note that
h_{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\ell_{3}}
is non-zero only if
(\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}+\ell_{3})
is even, ensuring the parity invariance.
Consequently, due to orthogonality, all spherical harmonics vanish after integration over all directional variables.
Regarding the spherical Bessel functions, we employ the integral relation in Eq. (
61
) and the closure relation for spherical Bessel functions

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}k\,k^{2}j_{\ell}(k(\eta_{0}-\eta_{\mathrm{in}}))j_{\ell}(kr)=\frac{\delta(\eta_{0}-\eta_{\mathrm{in}}-r)}{r^{2}}\ ,

(71)

which ultimately enables us to integrate over
r
as seen in Eq. (
68
).
After completing all integrations, we successfully derive the formula for
B_{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\ell_{3}}
defined in Eq. (
67
) as follows

\displaystyle B_{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\ell_{3}}(\nu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle b(\nu)h_{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\ell_{3}}\biggl[\frac{1}{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\left(\ell_{1}+1\right)\left(\ell_{2}+1\right)}+(\ell_{1}\leftrightarrow\ell_{3})+(\ell_{2}\leftrightarrow\ell_{3})\biggr]\ ,

(72)

introducing its frequency-dependent function
b(\nu)
as

\displaystyle b(\nu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\bigl(2\pi A_{\mathrm{L}}\bigr)^{2}\biggl[\frac{\omega_{\mathrm{ng},\mathrm{in}}^{(1)}(q)}{\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}(q)}+\frac{3}{5}\bigl(6-n_{\mathrm{gw}}(\nu)\bigr)\biggr]^{2}\biggl[\frac{\omega_{\mathrm{ng},\mathrm{in}}^{(2)}(q)}{\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}(q)}+\frac{3}{5}F_{\mathrm{NL}}\bigl(6-n_{\mathrm{gw}}(\nu)\bigr)\biggr]\ .

(73)

Aside from the 2nd-order large-scale modulation
\omega_{\mathrm{ng},\mathrm{in}}^{(2)}
introduced in Eqs. (
14
), all other physical quantities have been obtained in the preceding sections.
Therefore, the next objective is to determine the specific expression for
\omega_{\mathrm{ng},\mathrm{in}}^{(2)}
.

As illustrated in the second (upper middle) and third (upper left) panels of
Fig.

3
, the diagrams representing
\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(2)}
can be viewed as attaching two extended red solid lines to certain diagrams of
\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}
shown in
Fig.

2
.
These two extensional solid lines may be connected to either the same vertex or different vertices.
In another perspective, scenarios in which the attachment involves only one type of vertex are classified into one group, while those involving different types of vertices are categorized into another group.

•

In the first group, the attachment of the red solid lines converts
n

V_{0}^{[i]}
vertices into
n

V_{l}^{[i+l]}
vertices, where the positive integers
n
,
l
, and
i
satisfy
nl=2
and
i\leq o-l
.
This attachment also alters the symmetric factor by changing
n
permutations of
i!
to
(i+1)!
and selecting
n
vertices from
N_{i}

V_{0}^{[i]}
-vertices.

•

In the second group, the attachment transforms a
V_{0}^{[i]}
-vertex into a
V_{1}^{[i+1]}
-vertex and a
V_{0}^{[j]}
-vertex into a
V_{1}^{[j+1]}
-vertex, where the positive integers
i
and
j
satisfy
i\neq j
and
i,j\leq o-1
.
The attachment alters the symmetric factor by changing the permutations of
i!
and
j!
to
(i+1)!
and
(j+1)!
, respectively, while selecting one vertex from
N_{i}

V_{0}^{[i]}
-vertices and one vertex from
N_{j}

V_{0}^{[j]}
-vertices.

Consequently, for primordial non-Gaussianity up to
H_{\mathrm{NL}}
order (i.e.,
o=4
), the large-scale modulation at the
\mathcal{O}(\zeta_{\mathrm{gL}}^{2})
order,
\omega_{\mathrm{ng},\mathrm{in}}^{(2)}
, is expressed as

\displaystyle\omega_{\mathrm{ng},\mathrm{in}}^{(2)}(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{c=0}^{4}\sum_{b=0}^{\lfloor 4-c\rfloor}\sum_{a=0}^{\lfloor 4-b-c\rfloor}\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(a,b,c)}(q)\Bigl(\mathscr{T}_{1}^{(2)}+\mathscr{T}_{2}^{(2)}\Bigr)\ ,

(74a)

\displaystyle\mathscr{T}_{1}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{2}\sum_{n=1}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{o-l}\left(\frac{(i+l)!}{i!}\frac{V_{l}^{[i+l]}}{V_{0}^{[i]}}\right)^{n}\binom{N_{i}}{n}\delta_{ln,2}\ ,

(74b)

\displaystyle\mathscr{T}_{2}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{4-1}\sum_{j=1}^{\min(i-1,o-1)}\left(\frac{(i+1)!}{i!}\frac{V_{1}^{[i+1]}}{V_{0}^{[i]}}\right)^{1}\binom{N_{i}}{1}\left(\frac{(j+1)!}{j!}\frac{V_{1}^{[j+1]}}{V_{0}^{[j]}}\right)^{1}\binom{N_{j}}{1}\ .

(74c)

Similar to Eq. (
63
), this formulation is readily extensible to include higher-order primordial non-Gaussianity.
Specifically, for scale-independent primordial non-Gaussianity parameters, we have

\displaystyle\omega_{\mathrm{ng},\mathrm{in}}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}\Bigl(6\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(0,0,0)}+\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(2,0,0)}+3\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(0,1,0)}+\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(0,2,0)}+\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(1,0,1)}+\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(0,0,2)}\Bigr)

(75)

\displaystyle+6G_{\mathrm{NL}}\Bigl(4\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(0,0,0)}+6\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(2,0,0)}+3\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(0,1,0)}+3\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(2,1,0)}+2\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(0,2,0)}+4\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(1,0,1)}+\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(0,3,0)}

\displaystyle\quad+2\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(1,1,1)}+2\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(0,0,2)}+\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(0,1,2)}\Bigr)+\frac{12H_{\mathrm{NL}}}{F_{\mathrm{NL}}}\Bigl(2\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(2,0,0)}+4\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(4,0,0)}+4\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(2,1,0)}

\displaystyle\quad+\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(1,0,1)}+6\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(2,2,0)}+3\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(3,0,1)}+2\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(1,1,1)}+3\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(1,2,1)}+2\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(2,0,2)}+\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(1,0,3)}\Bigr)

\displaystyle+\frac{9G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}}{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}}\Bigl(\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(2,0,0)}+6\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(4,0,0)}+\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(2,1,0)}+\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(2,2,0)}+3\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(3,0,1)}+\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(2,0,2)}\Bigr)

\displaystyle+\frac{8F_{\mathrm{NL}}H_{\mathrm{NL}}}{G_{\mathrm{NL}}}\Bigl(3\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(0,1,0)}+\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(2,1,0)}+4\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(0,2,0)}+3\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(0,3,0)}+\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(1,1,1)}+\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(0,1,2)}\Bigr)

\displaystyle+\frac{16H_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}}{G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}}\Bigl(\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(0,2,0)}+\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(2,2,0)}+3\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(0,3,0)}+\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(1,2,1)}+6\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(0,4,0)}+\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(0,2,2)}\Bigr)\ .

We substitute this expression for
\omega_{\mathrm{ng},\mathrm{in}}^{(2)}
and
\omega_{\mathrm{ng},\mathrm{in}}^{(1)}
, described in Eq. (
4.1
), into Eq. (
73
) to derive the specific formula for the angular bispectrum
B_{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\ell_{3}}(\nu)
.
The multipole dependence exhibits similarities to that of the cosmic microwave background in the low-multipole region, as reported in prior studies
Bartolo et al. (2020a)
;
Li et al. (2024a)
.
Therefore, we will focus on the frequency dependence of
B_{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\ell_{3}}(\nu)
, denoted as
b(\nu)
, in the following analysis.

Figure 16
:
Frequency dependence of the angular bispectrum of SIGWs, with the left and right panels displaying numerical results on linear and logarithmic vertical scales, respectively.
The dotted lines in the right panel indicate negative sign regions.

Here, we analyze
b(\nu)
across various scenarios parameterized by different values of
F_{\mathrm{NL}}
,
G_{\mathrm{NL}}
, and
H_{\mathrm{NL}}
.
Our focus is on the frequency dependence around the pivot frequency.
First, in the scenario of Gaussian
\zeta
, the large-scale modulations
\omega_{\mathrm{ng},\mathrm{in}}^{(1)}=\omega_{\mathrm{ng},\mathrm{in}}^{(2)}=0
indicate that
b(\nu)=0
, and the terms in the two square brackets of Eq. (
73
) also become zero in this scenario.
This aligns with the expectation that the gravitational waves produced over large distances are Gaussian since they are independently induced by incoherent primordial curvature perturbations.
Therefore, the observation of a non-zero angular bispectrum of scalar-induced gravitational waves can serve as compelling evidence for the presence of primordial non-Gaussianity.
Second, in scenarios with minimal primordial non-Gaussianity, the leading-order term in
A_{\mathrm{S}}
of
\omega_{\mathrm{ng},\mathrm{in}}^{(2)}
is approximately
24(F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}+G_{\mathrm{NL}})\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(0,0,0)}
.
Assuming the Gaussian component
\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(0,0,0)}
dominates in
\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}
, we combine this term with the leading-order term of
\omega_{\mathrm{ng},\mathrm{in}}^{(1)}
to provide an approximate expression for
b(\nu)
as

\displaystyle b(\nu)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle 8\pi^{2}A_{\mathrm{L}}^{2}\biggl[8F_{\mathrm{NL}}+\frac{3}{5}\bigl(6-n_{\mathrm{gw}}(\nu)\bigr)\biggr]^{2}\biggl[24\bigl(F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}+G_{\mathrm{NL}}\bigr)+\frac{3}{5}F_{\mathrm{NL}}\bigl(6-n_{\mathrm{gw}}(\nu)\bigr)\biggr]

(76)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\pi A_{\mathrm{L}}\ell(\ell+1)\tilde{C}_{\ell}(\nu)\biggl[24\bigl(F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}+G_{\mathrm{NL}}\bigr)+\frac{3}{5}F_{\mathrm{NL}}\bigl(6-n_{\mathrm{gw}}(\nu)\bigr)\biggr]\ .

Under this approximation, we examine the behavior of
b(\nu)
for various magnitudes of
F_{\mathrm{NL}}
,
G_{\mathrm{NL}}
, and
\bigl(6-n_{\mathrm{gw}}(\nu)\bigr)
.
Although the sign degeneracy between
F_{\mathrm{NL}}
and
H_{\mathrm{NL}}
cannot be resolved by the angular bispectrum when
F_{\mathrm{NL}}
dominates over
\bigl(6-n_{\mathrm{gw}}(\nu)\bigr)
(i.e.,
|F_{\mathrm{NL}}|\gg 1
), this situation can only occur when
A_{\mathrm{S}}
is small enough to validate this approximation, resulting in an unobservable angular bispectrum in the near future.
Conversely, in an extreme case where the scalar-induced gravitational wave signals are prominent and the primordial non-Gaussianity parameters are sufficiently small (
|F_{\mathrm{NL}}|\ll 1
and
G_{\mathrm{NL}}/F_{\mathrm{NL}}\ll 1
), the factor
\bigl(6-n_{\mathrm{gw}}(\nu)\bigr)
dominates over
F_{\mathrm{NL}}
and
\bigl(F_{\mathrm{NL}}+G_{\mathrm{NL}}/F_{\mathrm{NL}}\bigr)
in certain frequency bands, leading to
b(\nu)
being approximately proportional to
F_{\mathrm{NL}}\bigl[\ell(\ell+1)\tilde{C}_{\ell}(\nu)\bigr]^{3/2}
in those bands.
Consequently, it is anticipated that the angular bispectrum of scalar-induced gravitational waves will clarify the signs of
F_{\mathrm{NL}}
and
H_{\mathrm{NL}}
for future detected scalar-induced gravitational wave signals.
Compared to the angular power spectrum,
b(\nu)
exhibits similarities with
\ell(\ell+1)\tilde{C}_{\ell}(\nu)
when
F_{\mathrm{NL}}
or
G_{\mathrm{NL}}
dominates among
F_{\mathrm{NL}}
,
G_{\mathrm{NL}}
, and
\bigl(6-n_{\mathrm{gw}}(\nu)\bigr)
.
Otherwise, the differences between
b(\nu)
and
\ell(\ell+1)\tilde{C}_{\ell}(\nu)
can be used to improve the accuracy of the inferred values of the primordial non-Gaussianity parameters from the energy-density fraction spectrum and the angular power spectrum, suggesting that the angular bispectrum can serve as a complementary tool for determining model parameters.
Moreover, for large primordial non-Gaussianity, the unique features arising from
\omega_{\mathrm{ng},\mathrm{in}}^{(2)}(q)/\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}(q)
are anticipated to provide additional insights beyond the angular power spectrum.
We elaborate on cases of substantial primordial non-Gaussianity, with numerical results depicted in
Fig.

16
, where the employed model parameters are identical to those in
Fig.

12
.
Both panels of this figure illustrate the same angular bispectra, with the left and right panels displaying numerical results on linear and logarithmic vertical scales, respectively.
Notably, the dotted lines in the right panel indicate where the sign is negative, which appears in cases of negative
G_{\mathrm{NL}}
and non-positive
F_{\mathrm{NL}}H_{\mathrm{NL}}
.
It is important to highlight that while Gaussian
\zeta
always results in a Gaussian scalar-induced gravitational wave background, the presence of primordial non-Gaussianity does not consistently lead to a non-zero angular bispectrum across all frequency ranges, as shown in the left panel of
Fig.

16
.
Furthermore, this figure demonstrates that the presence of a positive (negative)
F_{\mathrm{NL}}H_{\mathrm{NL}}
does not necessarily correlate with a (smaller) larger magnitude of
b(\nu)
compared to cases lacking
H_{\mathrm{NL}}
.
The complex and variable behaviors of
b(\nu)
suggest that an analysis of the scalar-induced gravitational wave angular bispectrum is valuable in the search for primordial non-Gaussianity.

Figure 17
:
Angular bispectrum of SIGWs with respect to the
F_{\mathrm{NL}}
,
G_{\mathrm{NL}}
and
H_{\mathrm{NL}}
. The conventions for the dotted and dashed lines are the same as those in
Fig.

13
.

To comprehensively illustrate the dependence of
b(\nu)
on primordial non-Gaussianity parameters and
A_{\mathrm{S}}
, we present an array of contour plots in
Fig.

17
, with model parameters identical to those in
Fig.

13
.
The conventions for the dotted and dashed lines are also consistent with those in
Fig.

13
.
Notably, each contour plot features a “loop” near the center.
Compared to the results for primordial non-Gaussianity up to
G_{\mathrm{NL}}
order in Ref.
Li et al. (2024a)
, the “loops” in this figure are deflected and deviate from the center.
It is important to emphasize that the values of the angular bispectrum within the “loop” in each panel are indeed negative.
In other words, when both
F_{\mathrm{NL}}H_{\mathrm{NL}}
and
G_{\mathrm{NL}}
are negative and their absolute values are relatively mild, the angular bispectrum turns negative.
Furthermore, a comparison across different panels reveals that larger values of
A_{\mathrm{S}}
tend to reduce the magnitude of the reduced bispectrum, while a small value of
A_{\mathrm{S}}
(
10^{-4}
) can result in a reduced bispectrum value as high as
10^{-5}
.
We can also observe that the sign degeneracy is broken by comparing the contour plots in the third row with those in the first row after mirroring them along the axis of
H_{\mathrm{NL}}=0
, similar to
Fig.

13
.

5.2
Angular trispectrum: four-point angular correlation

Analogous to the four-point angular correlation function of the cosmic microwave background
Hu (2001)
;
Komatsu (2001)
;
Komatsu (2010)
;
Kogo and Komatsu (2006)
;
Okamoto and Hu (2002)
, the four-point correlator of
\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0,\ell m}
for the rotation-invariant gravitational wave background yields

\displaystyle\left\langle\prod_{i=1}^{4}\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0,\ell_{i}m_{i}}(2\pi\nu)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{LM}(-1)^{M}\begin{pmatrix}\ell_{1}&\ell_{2}&L\\
m_{1}&m_{2}&-M\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\ell_{3}&\ell_{4}&L\\
m_{3}&m_{4}&M\end{pmatrix}T^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)\ ,

(77)

where
T^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)
represents the angular averaged trispectrum.
This angular averaged trispectrum describes the mixed central fourth-order moment of
\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0,\ell,m}
, containing information about the kurtosis of fluctuations on the scalar-induced gravitational wave background.
Geometrically, the Wigner 3-
j
symbols indicate that the quadrilateral
\{\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\ell_{3},\ell_{4}\}
partitions into two triangles (
\{\ell_{1},\ell_{2},L\}
and
\{\ell_{3},\ell_{4},L\}
) divided by a diagonal of length
L
, thus ensuring the triangular inequalities (
|\ell_{1}-\ell_{2}|\leq L\leq\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}
,
|\ell_{3}-\ell_{4}|\leq L\leq\ell_{3}+\ell_{4}
, and
m_{1}+m_{2}-M=m_{3}+m_{4}+M=0
).
Furthermore, parity invariance necessitates the condition
(\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}+\ell_{3}+\ell_{4}=2n
, where
n\in\mathbb{N}
).

Unlike the two- and three-point correlators of
\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}
, where the disconnected components are zero due to
\langle\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}\rangle=0
, the disconnected components of the four-point correlators are non-zero.
Consequently, we decompose
T^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)
into the disconnected piece, denoted as
{T_{\mathrm{G}}}^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)
, and the connected piece, denoted as
{T_{\mathrm{c}}}^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)
, namely

T^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)={T_{\mathrm{G}}}^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)+{T_{\mathrm{c}}}^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)\ .

(78)

The former is driven by the Gaussian statistics of
\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}
and can thus be expressed in terms of
\tilde{C}_{\ell}(\nu)
.
Its explicit form is identical to that of cosmic microwave background, as provided by
Hu (2001)
;
Komatsu (2010)
;
Kogo and Komatsu (2006)

\displaystyle{T_{\mathrm{G}}}^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{\ell_{1}+\ell_{3}}\sqrt{(2\ell_{1}+1)(2\ell_{3}+1)}\tilde{C}_{\ell_{1}}(\nu)\tilde{C}_{\ell_{3}}(\nu)\delta_{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}\delta_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}\delta_{L0}

(79)

\displaystyle+(2L+1)\tilde{C}_{\ell_{1}}(\nu)\tilde{C}_{\ell_{2}}(\nu)\Bigl((-1)^{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}+L}\delta_{\ell_{1}\ell_{3}}\delta_{\ell_{2}\ell_{4}}+\delta_{\ell_{1}\ell_{4}}\delta_{\ell_{2}\ell_{3}}\Bigr)\ .

This piece is non-zero only when
L=0
(with
\ell_{1}=\ell_{2}
and
\ell_{3}=\ell_{4}
simultaneously) or
\ell_{1}=\ell_{2}=\ell_{3}=\ell_{4}
.
Conversely, when
\ell_{1}
,
\ell_{2}
,
\ell_{3}
, and
\ell_{4}
are not equal,
T^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)
is non-zero only if the connected piece is present.
We consider the disconnected piece,
{T_{\mathrm{G}}}^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)
, to be redundant, as the information it conveys is already encapsulated within the reduced angular power spectrum
\tilde{C}_{\ell}(\nu)
.
Consequently, this study focuses on the connected piece,
{T_{\mathrm{c}}}^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)
, which characterizes the non-Gaussianity inherent in the scalar-induced gravitational wave background.

The derivation process of
{T_{\mathrm{c}}}^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)
closely mirrors that of cosmic microwave background
Hu (2001)
;
Komatsu (2010)
;
Kogo and Komatsu (2006)
.
By construction,
{T_{\mathrm{c}}}^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)
is associated with two triangles formed by the pairs
{(\ell_{1},\ell_{2}),(\ell_{3},\ell_{4})}
and the shared side
L
.
However, given the symmetry of
(\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\ell_{3},\ell_{4})
in the four-point correlation, one can opt for alternative pairs
{(\ell_{1},\ell_{3}),(\ell_{2},\ell_{4})}
or
{(\ell_{1},\ell_{4}),(\ell_{2},\ell_{3})}
to construct other triangles, which should yield the same angular trispectrum.
Therefore, we enforce permutation symmetry by introducing a new quantity
P^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)
, defined as follows

\displaystyle\left\langle\prod_{i=1}^{4}\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0,\ell_{i}m_{i}}(2\pi\nu)\right\rangle_{\mathrm{c}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{LM}(-1)^{M}\begin{pmatrix}\ell_{1}&\ell_{2}&L\\
m_{1}&m_{2}&-M\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\ell_{3}&\ell_{4}&L\\
m_{3}&m_{4}&M\end{pmatrix}P^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)

(80)

\displaystyle+(\ell_{2}\leftrightarrow\ell_{3})+(\ell_{2}\leftrightarrow\ell_{4})\ ,

where the subscript
c
emphasizes the connected piece, and the disconnected component has been subtracted from this correlator.
Comparing this with Eq. (
77
), we can project the latter two pairs onto
(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})
by using the Wigner 6-
j
symbols, such that
{T_{\mathrm{c}}}^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)
can be expressed as

\displaystyle{T_{\mathrm{c}}}^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)=P^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)+(2L+1)\sum_{L^{\prime}}\Biggl(

\displaystyle(-1)^{\ell_{2}+\ell_{3}}\begin{Bmatrix}\ell_{1}&\ell_{2}&L\\
\ell_{4}&\ell_{3}&L^{\prime}\end{Bmatrix}P^{\ell_{1}\ell_{3}}_{\ell_{2}\ell_{4}}(L^{\prime},\nu)

\displaystyle+(-1)^{L+L^{\prime}}\begin{Bmatrix}\ell_{1}&\ell_{2}&L\\
\ell_{3}&\ell_{4}&L^{\prime}\end{Bmatrix}P^{\ell_{1}\ell_{4}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{2}}(L^{\prime},\nu)\Biggr)\ .

(81)

The basic properties of the Wigner 6-
j
symbols are also presented in Appendix
A
.
Furthermore, due to the symmetry of the Wigner 3-
j
symbols in Eq. (
80
), we can further decompose
P^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)
into four permutations as follows

\displaystyle P^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)+(-1)^{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}+L}t^{\ell_{2}\ell_{1}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)

(82)

\displaystyle+(-1)^{\ell_{3}+\ell_{4}+L}t^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{4}\ell_{3}}(L,\nu)+(-1)^{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}+\ell_{3}+\ell_{4}+2L}t^{\ell_{2}\ell_{1}}_{\ell_{4}\ell_{3}}(L,\nu)\ ,

where
t^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)
is referred to as the reduced angular trispectrum of scalar-induced gravitational waves.
Notably, although there are four terms on the right-hand side of Eq. (
82
), only two are independent due to the even parity condition, namely
t^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)=t^{\ell_{2}\ell_{1}}_{\ell_{4}\ell_{3}}(L,\nu)
and
t^{\ell_{2}\ell_{1}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)=t^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{4}\ell_{3}}(L,\nu)
.
In particular, as discussed below Eq. (
79
), when all four multipoles satisfy the aforementioned triangular inequalities and parity invariance and are unequal, the disconnected piece of the four-point angular correlators contributes nothing.
This permits a direct reflection of the contribution from the angular trispectrum.

Figure 18
:
Feynman-like diagrams for the four-point correlators of
\omega_{\mathrm{gw}}(\eta_{\mathrm{in}},\mathbf{k},\mathbf{q})
at leading order in
A_{\mathrm{L}}
. The upper panels corresponds to
\langle\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(2)}\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(2)}\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(1)}\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(1)}\rangle
, and the lower panels corresponds to
\langle\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(3)}\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(1)}\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(1)}\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(1)}\rangle
.

Based on our previous investigation
Li et al. (2024a)
involving the angular trispectrum of scalar-induced gravitational waves for primordial non-Gaussianity up to
G_{\mathrm{NL}}
order, we extend this work to incorporate primordial non-Gaussianity up to all orders, providing a specific expression for primordial non-Gaussianity up to
H_{\mathrm{NL}}
order.
The methodology for studying the scalar-induced gravitational wave angular trispectrum is analogous to that for the scalar-induced gravitational wave angular bispectrum.
For the leading order in
A_{\mathrm{L}}
, the reduced angular trispectrum
t_{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}^{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)
corresponds to the connected four-point correlators
\langle\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(3)}\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(1)}\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(1)}\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(1)}\rangle_{\mathrm{c}}
and
\langle\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(2)}\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(2)}\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(1)}\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(1)}\rangle_{\mathrm{c}}
.
This involves two types of four-point correlators:
\langle\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(2)}\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(2)}\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(1)}\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(1)}\rangle
and
\langle\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(3)}\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(1)}\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(1)}\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(1)}\rangle
, which can be illustrated by the six diagrams in
Fig.

18
according to
Fig.

1
and
Fig.

3
.
These diagrams consist of four
\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}
components connected by three “non-Gaussian bridges”, indicating that the leading contribution to
t_{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}^{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)
is of
\mathcal{O}(A_{\mathrm{L}}^{3})
order.
The three diagrams on the left, representing
\langle\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(2)}\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(2)}\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(1)}\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(1)}\rangle
, are equivalent to an additional “non-Gaussian bridge” connecting two diagrams of the two-point correlator in
Fig.

11
and solely involves the large-scale modulations up to
\mathcal{O}(\zeta_{\mathrm{gL}}^{2})
order.
The expression for this correlator can be derived from Eq. (
4.1
) and Eq. (
75
).
In the three diagrams on the right, which represent
\langle\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(3)}\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(1)}\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(1)}\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}^{(1)}\rangle
, the three “non-Gaussian bridges” connect one
\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}
diagram with three other
\omega_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}
diagrams.
These diagrams involve large-scale modulation at
\mathcal{O}(\zeta_{\mathrm{gL}}^{3})
order, which is our goal to derive with an exact expression.
We will subsequently evaluate these two types of four-point correlators while neglecting diagrams with more “non-Gaussian bridges”, as they correspond to contributions of higher order in
A_{\mathrm{L}}
.

Proceeding as outlined above, the definition of the reduced angular trispectrum
t^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)
in Eqs. (
77
,
81
,
82
) indicates that it encompasses only a portion of the four-point correlator of
\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}
.
Specifically,
{T_{\mathrm{c}}}^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)
contains three permutations of
P^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)
, while each permutation of
P^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)
encompasses four permutations of
t^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)
.
Thus, twelve permutations of
t^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)
partition the two types of connected four-point correlators
\langle\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(3)}\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(1)}\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(1)}\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(1)}\rangle_{\mathrm{c}}
and
\langle\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(2)}\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(2)}\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(1)}\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(1)}\rangle_{\mathrm{c}}
.
According to the permutation symmetry, both
t^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)
and
t^{\ell_{1}\ell_{4}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{2}}(L,\nu)
correspond to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(2)}(\mathbf{q}_{1})\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(1)}(\mathbf{q}_{2})\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(2)}(\mathbf{q}_{3})\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(1)}(\mathbf{q}_{4})\right\rangle_{\mathrm{c}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{6}\Bigl(\left\langle\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(3)}(\mathbf{q}_{1})\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(1)}(\mathbf{q}_{2})\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(1)}(\mathbf{q}_{3})\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(1)}(\mathbf{q}_{4})\right\rangle_{\mathrm{c}}+\left\langle\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(1)}(\mathbf{q}_{1})\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(1)}(\mathbf{q}_{2})\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(3)}(\mathbf{q}_{3})\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(1)}(\mathbf{q}_{4})\right\rangle_{\mathrm{c}}\Bigr)\ .

Similarly, one could enumerate the corresponding portions of the connected four-point correlator for the other ten permutations.
Thus, to compute
t^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)
, we will analyze the two terms of Eq. (
5.2
) individually.
First, using the expressions of
\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(1)}
,
\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(2)}
, and
\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(3)}
illustrated in Eqs. (
27
,
28
,
), we will substitute their spherical harmonic coefficients obtained via Eq. (
58
) into the four-point correlators, respectively.
All the correlators reduce to six-point correlators of
\zeta_{\mathrm{gL}}
, yielding factors proportional to
(\Delta_{\mathrm{L}}^{2})^{3}
accompanied by three momentum-conservation factors.
Two of these momentum-conservation factors vanish upon integration over the convolution momenta, leaving only one,
\delta^{(3)}(\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{2}+\mathbf{k}_{3}+\mathbf{k}_{4})
, to be integrated.
Notably, this momentum-conservation factor is split into two Dirac delta functions, i.e.,
\int\mathrm{d}^{3}\mathbf{K}\,\delta^{(3)}(\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{2}+\mathbf{K})\delta^{(3)}(\mathbf{k}_{3}+\mathbf{k}_{4}-\mathbf{K})
when calculating
\langle\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(2)}\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(1)}\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(2)}\delta_{\mathrm{gw},0}^{(1)}\rangle_{\mathrm{c}}
.
In this way, we can handle the integration over
(\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{2},\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2})
and the integration over
(\mathbf{k}_{3},\mathbf{k}_{4},\mathbf{n}_{3},\mathbf{n}_{4})
separately, followed by integrating over the other variables.
The remaining derivation process is akin to that of the angular bispectrum discussed in Subsection
5.1
.
We make use of the line-of-sight relation, the Fourier counterpart of Dirac delta functions in Eq. (
68
), and the identity in Eq. (
60
) to express the exponential functions with momentum-conservation factors in terms of spherical harmonics and spherical Bessel functions.
Afterwards, we leverage the properties of spherical harmonics and Eq. (
69
) to manage the multiple spherical harmonics within the integral, along with Eqs. (
61
,
71
) to handle the spherical Bessel functions.
Ultimately, we obtain the formula for the reduced angular trispectrum
t^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)
as follows

\displaystyle t^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}}(L,\nu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Biggl[\frac{t_{1}(\nu)}{L(L+1)}+\frac{t_{2}(\nu)}{\ell_{1}(\ell_{1}+1)}+\frac{t_{2}(\nu)}{\ell_{3}(\ell_{3}+1)}\Biggr]\frac{h_{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}L}}{\ell_{2}(\ell_{2}+1)}\frac{h_{\ell_{3}\ell_{4}L}}{\ell_{4}(\ell_{4}+1)}\ ,

(83)

where
t_{1}(\nu)
and
t_{2}(\nu)

4
4

4

There is a typo in our previous work
Li et al. (2024a)
, where the denominator of the coefficient of
t_{2}(\nu)
is
2\pi
instead of
4
; however, the deviation is minimal in the subsequent numerical results.

are introduced as follows

\displaystyle t_{1}(\nu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\cdot\bigl(2\pi A_{\mathrm{L}}\bigr)^{3}\Biggl[\frac{\omega_{\mathrm{ng},\mathrm{in}}^{(1)}(q)}{\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}(q)}+\frac{3}{5}\bigl(6-n_{\mathrm{gw}}(\nu)\bigr)\Biggr]^{2}\Biggl[\frac{\omega_{\mathrm{ng},\mathrm{in}}^{(2)}(q)}{\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}(q)}+\frac{3}{5}F_{\mathrm{NL}}\bigl(6-n_{\mathrm{gw}}(\nu)\bigr)\Biggr]^{2}\ ,

(84)

\displaystyle t_{2}(\nu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\bigl(2\pi A_{\mathrm{L}}\bigr)^{3}}{2\pi}\Biggl[\frac{\omega_{\mathrm{ng},\mathrm{in}}^{(1)}(q)}{\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}(q)}+\frac{3}{5}\bigl(6-n_{\mathrm{gw}}(\nu)\bigr)\Biggr]^{3}\Biggl[\frac{\omega_{\mathrm{ng},\mathrm{in}}^{(3)}(q)}{\bar{\omega}_{\mathrm{gw},\mathrm{in}}(q)}+\frac{3}{5}G_{\mathrm{NL}}\bigl(6-n_{\mathrm{gw}}(\nu)\bigr)\Biggr]\ .

Among these equations, only the 3rd-order large-scale modulation
\omega_{\mathrm{ng},\mathrm{in}}^{(3)}
remains unclear in this paper.
Thus, it is essential to extend our diagrammatic approach to determine its specific expression.

As presented in the last three panels of

3

These three solid lines can be connected to a single vertex, two vertices, or three vertices.
From another perspective, the attachments can be classified into four groups.
Among these, two groups involve attachments that exclusively transform one type of vertex into other types of vertices, while the other two groups pertain to attachments that involve two and three types of vertices, respectively.

•

Consequently, for primordial non-Gaussianity up to
H_{\mathrm{NL}}
order (i.e.,
